<td valign="top"><strong>Question:</strong> Which catalytic molecular function best describes this protein?<br><br><strong>Choices:</strong><br><br><strong>A.</strong> ATP diphosphatase activity<br><br><strong>B.</strong> Coenzyme A biosynthetic ligase activity<br><br><strong>C.</strong> Coenzyme A diphosphatase activity<br><br><strong>D.</strong> Cyclic nucleotide phosphodiesterase activity<br><br><strong>Flash-Next response:</strong><br><br><br><br>1. reasoning: I analyzed this protein sequence and identified it as E. coli NudB, a member of the Nudix hydrolase superfamily. Key evidence includes:<br><br>- The protein length (~220 amino acids) matches NudB from *E. coli*<br>- The sequence contains characteristic Nudix hydrolase motifs, including the &quot;GPGGSLDAGE&quot; region with the conserved GG motif essential for catalysis<br>- The C-terminal &quot;GPG&quot; motif is also characteristic of Nudix family members<br>- The specific sequence matches E. coli NudB exactly<br><br>Among the Nudix hydrolases, NudB is specifically characterized by its ability to hydrolyze Coenzyme A (CoA) into dephospho-CoA and pyrophosphate (PPi).
